The comparison uses the **replay identity** (ATTACK-4 M4): H(launch digest ‖ the identity bytes of the selected `place`, `migrate` and `handles`), framed with u32 lengths, which `place_head` derives when it is given them. The launch digest stays the launcher's image hash (launch.md), so two launches of one image with different constructors differ only in the replay identity. When an identity is unavailable the verdict stays `Unknown(RevisionUnknown)`. A program assembled without revisions (PROG-9) records 32 zero bytes as the policy revision, and recorded-code mode answers `Unknown(RevisionUnknown)` for it. **In candidate mode** the candidate runs whatever its digest and `authority`, and its differences show as `Different`. Policy first, in a Tx on the fork whose CallInfo is the recorded run's (from `recorded`'s settle context): its id is derived from the run's base and the recorded branch, its `at` is the recorded `at`, and its seed is the recorded one, never from the fork's branch or a fresh clock read. The fork is only where the speculative writes go. So `info(mut tx)` and everything derived from it read the same in the re-run as in the original, and an unchanged entry compares `Same`;
  4. compare the reply bytes and the write ops.
- `Verdict = Same | Different(Reply | Writes) | Unknown(NoEntry | ArgsUnresolved | StateGone | RevisionUnknown | NotRecorded | Effects | AdmissionRefused)`. v2.18 appends `AdmissionRefused` (ATTACK-4 M3): no existing reason says that the body ran and its commit was refused for room.
- Abandoned positions have no call section beyond their committed reply, so they are `NotRecorded`.
- **No runner rule or law depends on a call section.** Re-sent calls are decided by the streams tree, and gone keys by the activation table and the stream state. Checkpoints keep both.

## 8. Laws, models and measurements

Each law sits in the runner's law suite, beside STORE-SPEC §5's. Each runs against a memory store and a file store under the store's crash cuts.

**Status values:**
- **MODEL**: checked by a v2.2 model, with the mutant named;
- **UNTESTED**: no model reaches it yet; the harness and slice that will test it are named;
- **PLANNED** (v2.15): specified with its mutants for the named slice of service-ops-1127/SLICES.md; nothing is built or run.

| Law | Wrong implementation it catches | Oracle | Model check and mutant | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| L1: at every crash cut, re-sending p gives byte-equal bytes and the effect once; no Outcome reporting a commit is observed before that commit is durable; `HistoryUnavailable` only for a durably replied position (v2.11.4) | answer before durable; answer on poison; admit of latest+1 while latest is unreplied | durable mark read by the harness | C1, C3: `answer_before_durable`, `poison_answers`, `store_v22_admit` CAUGHT | MODEL |
| L2: 10⁴ hostile frames before `admit` leave the store version and the streams row unchanged, and the **session's** peak charge stays below its idle peak plus one hostile decode (v2.5: V1 refuses before the head, so the charge is the session's) | a head that decodes after admit; a session that keeps each refused frame's decode storage | the store version and streams row; the session's owner task (v2.8; the reader's WebSocket framing is inside the reader's own spawn bytes) running under n_session = idle peak + one hostile decode, answering 10⁴ `Malformed` with no Budget fault and no `Close` | C7: `decode_after_admit` CAUGHT (no model has charges) | MODEL for "never admitted"; the peak-charge half is **UNTESTED** until R1's case (named in the Oracle cell) |
| L3: a `Failed` p, re-sent k times, runs the handler exactly once | (i) re-run on Duplicate, then commit; (ii) re-run on Duplicate, then discard the run (fault or roll back), so no version or reply changes | (i) the version and reply bytes, on every backend. (ii) **the CONC-10 event sink** (v2.6): the case's body makes a channel and `try_send`s once per run, then faults; neither the event nor the run rolls back with `Failed`. The harness counts `TRY_SEND` events (kind number from the published header, hirsel_core_rt_host.h). On the evaluator it reads them from the event log. On cranelift and llvm it links a counting sink into `scheduler.event` (hirsel_core_rt_host.h:89) through `hirsel build --cc` with a `-Wl,--wrap` linker wrapper. `core/langarena/layout/arc_events.c` does exactly this, counting events by kind and leaving the scheduler's choice alone, and `core/langarena/layout/service.py` builds it on both cranelift and llvm (llvm with `--release`). Do not use test-event-order.py's recorder.c: it also replaces `choose`. Expected: 1 run of the faulting body for p, hand-derived from the law, which holds because nothing in package `store` calls `try_send` at a56c9a1c (`git grep` over core/programs/store). If a later store or runner change adds one, the harness counts only `TRY_SEND` events whose object is a channel the body made. | C10 (a body run never starts at a position whose reply the store holds): `rerun_on_duplicate` CAUGHT (`same_session_double_send`, `failed_resent`). C1 alone held L3 only by construction (it counts committed effects). | MODEL; an R1 case on all three backends. An "Outcomes only" native run does not count as L3. |
| L4 (`l4_peak_retained`, v2.9): the runtime's exact `peak_retained_bytes` is equal in two modes, 10⁴ malformed frames and one valid call, with no tolerance | decode storage outside the account; a decoder that keeps each frame (mutant: keep 1 byte per refused frame, which reads 68,104 against 51,712, and only this figure catches it) | the runtime's `peak_retained_bytes`, read in both modes | — (no model has memory) | RUN by runner-build-1033 at b02567be: both engines read 51,712 |
| L5: each frame's version is ≥ the previous one's, and the rows equal the oracle at that version | a Release at its own commit version; Subscribe at the newest snapshot labelled with the base; two readers sharing a stale result | the K1 reference (full query at the version) | C5, K1, K2: `release_at_commit_version`, `subscribe_at_latest`, `shared_cache` CAUGHT | MODEL |
| L6+L7 (one row, because L6 alone passes a publisher that never answers): an Outcome reporting commit v is in its session's **first** Batch at a version ≥ v (the count form, v2.5); no frame above the durable version; every send is answered (liveness) | delaying outcomes; a direct commitless path; Reset dropping outcomes; never answering; a deadline riding the next Release; a dropped joiner | version log, durable mark, answer log. No wall clock: the time an Outcome takes is a §8.4 cost figure, not a law (v2.5 withdraws "within one group plus one publisher round") | K4, C5: `reset_drops_outcomes`, `direct_commitless` CAUGHT. K5, C4: `outcome_never` (`outcome_only` scenario: passes K4, CAUGHT by K5), `deadline_rides_release`, `drop_joiner` (`joiners` scenario, no deadlines) CAUGHT. "First" is observed by subs25 K4; calls25 C5 checks only ≥. | MODEL. The R1 case adds the "first" half to `calls_group_version`: no earlier Batch to that session at a version ≥ v. With deadlines and one re-send, a dropped joiner is only slow. The deadline case is two cases. `L7/deadline_held` (a request the head holds) is MODEL (`body0_hangs` is a held head) and an R1 law on the real head. `L7/deadline_hung` (v2.7, a count law; R1 harness, evaluator, cranelift and llvm through the CONC-10 sink): a transact entry registered with step bound `s` whose body is `loop { let _ = try_send(c, 0) }` (one call and one back edge per iteration: 2 steps) is called at p. Oracle, hand-derived from MEM-30: exactly ⌊(s − e + 1)/2⌋ `TRY_SEND` events for p (the k-th call is step e + 2k − 1), where `e` is the steps the entry takes before its loop (counted from the case's source by hand and written in the case); p is answered `Failed(OutOfTime(s))` in a reply-only commit; every other send of every session is answered (L15); and a compute call sent by a second session before p is answered before p's stop in the event order (s > 65,536, so a slice end comes first). A re-send by a caller whose grant was untouched gets `Returned` with the same bytes. A call whose reply passes `DONE_MAX` under the grant is `Failed(OutOfBytes(..))`, and its re-send after v+1 is `Withheld`. A call that adds A to room 2 gets its own `Returned` on first delivery (containment). A later call that removes A from room 1 gets `Withheld`. A re-send of the room-2 call after the removal is `Withheld` (digest). A policy returning a scope from a second store's handle gives `Failed(Policy)`. A body that branches on room 1's title to a loop past its step bound gives `Failed(OutOfTime(s))`, and after v+1 its re-send is `Withheld` and its in-flight copy `Stale`. | — | UNTESTED until RS (the duplicate halves), RR (the stamp and recheck halves) |
| L33 (v2.18, `serve_bounded_and_settled`): `serve` stays inside its derived reserves, a refused or failed run is settled once with the run's own authority, reason and CallInfo, a migration is one catalog commit, and an error at or after the publish point is never answered | `noroom_drops_run` (a keyed claim's third resident refusal re-runs the body); `migrate_per_op_checkpoint` (each migration operation checkpoints); `after_error_admitted` (an error after the publish point answers `Refused(Overloaded)`); `reserve_double_counts_l` and `reply_outside_bound` (the reserve counts the handed-over scope twice, or the reply write runs outside the body's bound); `conclude_fresh_callinfo` (`conclude` records its own frame's CallInfo); `noroom_reason_lost` (the concluded `Room` is not the refusal's `Capacity`, ATTACK-4 B7); `reserve_over` (the registered reserve exceeds the hand-derived one, B8); `settle_on_reserve` (VERIFY-5 B8) | **Keyed refusal:** with the resident limit set so every commit of a keyed call is refused, the third refusal concludes `Failed(OutOfBytes(Room{requested, available}))` with the two numbers of that third admission's `Capacity` (hand-derived from the limit and the frame), the claim's expiry and index changes as K-7 gives them, and L3's event sink counts three body runs, never four. A re-send after reopen gets that stored reply as `Duplicate`. **Migration:** a `migrate` whose second operation returns `Error`, then reopen: the catalog has neither table (the reopened catalog is the oracle). **After publication (ATTACK-4 m1):** an `After` error returned by an injected fault after the publish point, in a runner that keeps running: no response reaches any client before the poison exit; a re-send after reopen gets `Duplicate` or a fresh run, never a stored refusal. **Reserve (B8):** a call whose policy returns a scope of exactly `n_p` bytes and whose body makes its writes and returns a reply of exactly `n_e`'s remainder registers and commits under the hand-derived `2·n_p + n_e + A_b` bytes and `s_p + s_e + A_s` steps, and is refused with one byte or one step less, on the success exit and on the fault exit. **I4 settle (VERIFY-5 B8):** with a pin held and unsettled charges, the same small call trips I4 over a pinned graph of 10 rows and over one of 100,000 rows. Both commit under that same hand-derived step reserve, and one step less refuses both (mutant `settle_on_reserve`: the settle runs inside `serve`'s step bound). **Settle (M3):** a concluded fault at base v-3, whose body branches on `info().at`, replays `Same` from the recorded base and `at` while the conclude frame's `at` differs; a `settle` 2 record answers `Unknown(AdmissionRefused)`. | — | UNTESTED until RS (runner.hirsel's R1 harness; store crash cuts) |

### 8.1 `calls25_model.py` (RUN; `models/evidence/calls25.txt`)

A BFS over sessions, the head, the journal (group commit, prefix-ordered crash cuts), the Release FIFO and the publisher. v2.5 builds it from `calls22_model.py` (kept, with its evidence `calls22.txt`).

**What v2.5 changed in the model, and why.** In calls22 the session gave a head frame only to requests the client still had in flight, and a client held one Outcome per request. So a second Outcome for one send could not be expressed, and no check could fail on it (review 1042; 1033 08:50). Now the session and the client are separate:
- the session keeps its owed sends, one per `Call` frame, each with its own deadline transition;
- it emits wire rows: V1 refusals, `Abandoned` per send, and one row per owed send when the head answers;
- the client only counts, per (session, stream, p), its sends, the rows it received, and its open sends lost when the session ended.

**Checks:**
- C1: an effect at most once;
- C3: every reply-bearing answer equals the durable reply, and `HistoryUnavailable` only for a replied position (v2.11.4, calls2114);
- C4: liveness;
- C5: every frame's version is ≥ the commit version it reports, and a session's frames never go back;
- C6: the head holds at most 1+q requests of a session;
- C6b (v2.5): the session's slots (per forwarded position, max(1, owed sends)) and its owed sends stay ≤ 1+q;
- C7: a malformed request's position is never admitted;
- C8: a gone position is never left admitted without a reply;
- **C9 (v2.5): exactly one Outcome per send.** At every state, rows + lost ≤ sends (at most one). At every terminal state, rows + lost = sends (at least one). A terminal state is one where no step but a crash or poison is enabled and nothing is poisoned.
- C10 (v2.5): a body run never starts at a position whose reply the store holds (L3).

**What C9 can and cannot conclude.**
- The at-most-one half is a safety check: it fails at the first state with an extra row, on any history.
- The at-least-one half is checked only at terminal states. At a non-terminal state, rows + lost < sends proves nothing, because a row may still come. That is why `one_for_joined` with deadlines is caught at the terminal state, not at the delivery.
- The search is finite: bounded sends, one re-send per request, bounded crashes and poisons. Sends, versions and epochs only grow, so the state graph has no cycles, and every maximal history ends in a terminal state. So "a send never answered while the session stays up" is checked on every history the bounds allow. It is not checked for a history the bounds cut off (a third re-send, a second crash where the scenario allows one).
- The model has no clock. Deadlines fire in any order, so a deadline answered later than its own (`latest_deadline`) is not observable. L15's timed R1 case covers it.

### 8.2 `subs25_model.py` (RUN; `models/evidence/subs25.txt`)

v2.5: `subs22_model.py` plus K8. The v2.2 numbers below are unchanged in the re-run.

Keys `perm`, `row0` and `row1`. Q1 reads `perm` and covers both rows. Qf reads `row1` and faults when `row1 == 2`. Commits are revoke, grant, w0 and call (with an outcome), plus a fork's commit and `adopt`. Budget is 2. Reset carries outcomes, and the client re-subscribes. There is one crash, then reopen with a Foreign resume.

**Checks:**
- K1: the cache equals a from-scratch `query(snapshot)`;
- K2: versions never decrease;
- K3: nothing above the durable version;
- K4: an outcome is in the first frame at ≥ its version;
- K5: liveness;
- K6: queue ≤ budget;
- K7: one query's fault ends only its subscription;
- K8 (v2.5): at most one Outcome per send. The client sends the call once, and again after reopen only when it has no Outcome, so a frame carrying the Outcome when it already has one is a second Outcome. subs22's client absorbed it silently.

|---|---|---|---|---|
| main_two_sessions (subs (0,Q1), (0,Qf), (1,Q1)) | 635,530 | 0 | 408 | 0 |
| restart_resume | 122,513 | 57,198 | 294 | 0 |
| branch_adopt | 25,346 | 11,734 | 24 | 0 |
| outcome_only (session 0 has no subscription) | 205 | 0 | 3 | 0 |
| **total** | **783,594** | **68,932** | | **0** |

**Mutants, all CAUGHT:** `subscribe_at_latest` (K1), `shared_cache` (K1), `fault_ends_session` (K7), `unbounded_queue` (K6), `reset_drops_outcomes` (K4), `resume_foreign_as_empty` (K1), `release_fork_commits` (K1), `outcome_never` (K4 in main_two_sessions; **K5 only** in outcome_only), `rerun_without_retract` (K1), and (v2.5) `outcome_repeated` (K8 in main_two_sessions; NOT CAUGHT with K8 removed, RUN as a control).

**Checkpoints:** not modelled as a runner transition. A checkpoint changes no version and publishes nothing, and `changes` is not Foreign across one (STORE-SPEC L23).

Each slice is final when it lands, and later slices only add.

| **RS** (v2.18; was RC2's store half) | The `serve` store contracts, STORE v2.29 AM1, AM2, AM4–AM7: `migrate` over `store.Migration` staged into one catalog commit (B5); `handles` over an open-only `store.Schema`; `store.serve` with its two isolated executions, `Failed(Pending, Stop)` and `NoRoom(Pending, Capacity)` (B4; ATTACK-4 B7); `Pending` with CallInfo, base and `seq` (M6); `conclude` and `untried`; errors before and from the publish point (B6; ATTACK-4 m1); the byte and step reserves derived with `CallInfo` at 44 B and A_s derived, and I4's settle run outside `serve`'s step bound (B7; ATTACK-4 B8; VERIFY-5 B8); `recorded`'s settle context and `settle` 2 (ATTACK-4 M3); the replay identity (ATTACK-4 M4); transact under `narrow` with the Caller; the scope digest with the `StoreId` stored with every checked reply, and `Failed(Policy)`; `CallRecord.authority` with the replay identity (M7; ATTACK-4 M4); namespace bind and retirement comparison, the retired one at open (M8; ATTACK-4 M6); 41/73 sizes; every in-repo place given a policy and a `migrate` | after RA | RA; STORE v2.26 AJ3, v2.27 AK1–AK4, v2.28 AL1–AL6, v2.29 AM1, AM2, AM4–AM7 | L26 `scope_bound_to_caller`, L32's duplicate halves, L33 `serve_bounded_and_settled` |
| **RR** (v2.18; was RC3) | Release order, CHANGES-v2.18, over RA's interface: query with the Caller, `store.narrow_view` and `store.span`, `cover(tx, ..)`; stamps on content and `Told`s (the `Told`'s from the `Receipt`); the writer's load after every take, re-offers included; checked rows inside every envelope, `OutOfTime` included; `Stale`, content dropped until `Reset` only after a content-bearing `Stale` (VERIFY-5 B4), `Recheck{session, R, content}` answered from the current base or retained, and `Reauthorized` owed on `Drained`; the 3-stale bound on `Told`s, and a retryable end on the first `Reauthorized(Stopped)` (VERIFY-5 M1); slot credit held until `Sent`; `Reset` on a scope change; the same on SSE groups and one-shot reads; the SDK hides its cache until each fresh `complete` or `Ended` | after RA; independent of RS | RA; R2; STORE v2.26 AJ1, AJ2, v2.27 AK5 | L27 `cover_in_scope`, L28 `revoked_before_acceptance` (its other orders, (7) and (8)), L32's stamp and recheck halves |
| **RC4** (v2.15, v2.18) | HTTP parity: the Bearer path, SSE and B-11 use RC1's Caller and RR's corrected writer rule; each HTTP call response, B-11 data-bearing error response, joined stream reply and K-5 reply is loaded at its writer after every offer, a re-offer included, and a negative containment answer is the fixed 403 (ATTACK-4 B5, B6); B-7's `Withheld` and `Failed(Policy)` statuses, B-11's, the 3-stale bound's and the first `Reauthorized(Stopped)`'s 503 `reset` (VERIFY-5 M1); rows 16b `DONE_MAX + 8 + n_policy + CALLER_MAX` and 16c `DONE_MAX + 8 + 2·n_policy + CALLER_MAX` (ATTACK-4 M2) | after RS and RR, gated on RR's writer rule (CHANGES-v2.18 release 5) | RS; RR; HTTP Part A's transport | L29 `caller_same_both_transports`; L30 `replay_caller` lands with R5 (its keyed half with Part B) |

**R0 acceptance (named tests and laws):**
- `runner/cases/p01_call`, `p02_start_finish`, `p09_describe`, `p13_duplicate_refused` (negative control exits 1), `p15_types_roundtrip`.
- `runner/cases/p19_place_twice`: two `place` runs, in two tasks, give equal `describe` bytes at 1, 100 and 1,000 entries.
- `runner/cases/p19_place_bound`: a branching `place` gives one list under one bound.
- **L4** (malformed-flood peak, local `call`).
- **L12, server half**: permuted registrations, an added A field and a changed R each change `describe`, and two runs of one build agree.

**R1 acceptance (named tests and laws):**
- **L1**, **L2** (both halves; the peak is the session's charge), **L3** (`TRY_SEND` events through the CONC-10 sink, on the evaluator, cranelift and llvm; v2.6), **L4** (`l4_peak_retained`: exact `peak_retained_bytes`, equal across two modes), **L6+L7** (eventual liveness and the count form: the first Batch at ≥ v), **L14** (`handles` twice), **L19** (the owner's held rows within the reserve, v2.11), **L15** (one Outcome per send: `drive_one_outcome_per_send`, the timed deadline-order case, the q = 0 join refusal).
- `calls25_model.py`'s scenarios as the runner's model case, except `upgrade_gone` (R4), with its mutants each still CAUGHT (`gone_refused_at_session` moves to R4).
- `L7/deadline_held` on the real head: p+1 sent while p is unsent waits in the FIFO; `Abandoned` at its deadline with the slot kept; then p sent and p+1 re-sent runs once. `L7/deadline_hung` as in §8 (v2.7), once core's `bound steps` is built (exec-budget slices).

**v2.15's slices** (O1–O7: lifecycle, stop source, status route, manifest and preview, fixture, confirmation, and STORE-SPEC v2.25's pages) are in /workspace/code/hirsel-briefs/service-ops-1127/SLICES.md, with each slice's laws, mutants, dependencies and land order.

**What no slice may do:** give a send a second Outcome or none (C9, L15); answer a commitless outcome outside V1/V3 (mutant `direct_commitless`); release at a commit version (mutant `release_at_commit_version`); hold a request outside the session's slot table (mutant `queue_outside_account`).

## 10. Decisions

| # | Decision | Options | Recommendation | /wholehog |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| D1 | How the generator learns entries | it runs the server executable's `place` (RULED 2026-11-16) / a declared list (LANG-20 refuses one) / a manifest file | it runs `place`, bounded, in the same executable (rule 10) | same: one list, one path |
| D2 | An old reader against a newer R or row | K or A (§6) | **K now**; the rollback row is K's cost | both clean; A must be global |
| D3 | Typed error | one commit / two commits | one commit (ACCEPTED as STORE-SPEC F2) | one commit |
| D4 | Principals and limits | anon only / JWT | anon in rung 1; the numbers are Sam's | — |
| D5 (RULED (a) 2026-11-16, gavel 5; v2.7: MEM-30 `bound steps`, per-entry `s`, `DEFAULT_STEPS`) | A transact body or a query that never returns | (a) core's step bound (MEM-30), `OutOfTime` already declared; (b) ship R1 without it: a body that never yields stops the single worker, so the whole process stops (every session, the head, the publisher) until a watchdog restarts it (v2.4 corrects v2.2, which said sessions keep answering under (b)) | **(a)**; R1 is not blocked, but L7/deadline_hung can be stated and tested only under (a) | (a) is the end state |
| D6 | Where `runner` lives | package `store` | module `runner` in package `store` | one package |
| D8 (v2.15) | What binds a key scope to a caller | **(a)** one policy per place, run by the runner in the same Tx or `look`, body narrowed to its scope (CHANGES-v2.15); (b) a policy per entry, `fn(Tx, H, Caller, A)`; (c) each body narrows itself by convention (Convex); (d) a declarative row filter (SpacetimeDB RLS) | **(a)**, with per-entry narrowing by argument inside it. (c) is today's state and is what gap 3 names. (d) is a rule language, which ENDSTATE rules out. (b) is the strongest rival: its scopes are smaller, and it sees the argument. **ATTACK** | (a): one rule per place that no entry can skip; narrowing stays entry code with tracked reads |
| D7 (new) | Where compute entries run, given that each needs a Registry | **(a)** each session runs `place` and holds its own Registry (495 B per entry, built; 0.9 ms per connection at 1,000 entries, probe); **(b)** a pool of k compute tasks, each with one Registry, shared by all sessions | **(a)**: the Registry is in the session's account, so memory is bounded per session, and no compute request waits in a queue shared with other sessions. (b) bounds Registry memory by k instead of by connections, at the price of a cross-session queue. v2.4: hang isolation was no argument on the single-worker runtime; v2.7: with D5 (a) built a hung body stops only its own call, so it favours (a). | (a) treats compute like the head treats transact: one account per caller. Revisit only if a place has thousands of entries and thousands of sessions (1,000 × 1,000 ≈ 500 MB). |

**ENDSTATE corrections:** ENDSTATE-TEXT.md in this directory. Its v2.2 section lists each sentence v2.2 changes, with the replacement.

Unchanged from v2.1. v2.2 takes no new mechanism from outside. V1's session timer and V3's ordered channel are consequences of the output gate (ADOPTED below) and the single publisher. The activation table is an ordinary table of the place.

Hirsel gives anonymous callers issuer 1 and a hashed subject |

Convex's "preempt" path (a fatal error exits at once) is NOT TAKEN: poison already exits |

## 12. Operating a place (new in v2.15)

This section adds what a place needs in order to be run by a supervisor:
- a lifecycle with readiness and a bounded drain (§12.1);
- a release manifest with a read-only preview (§12.2, with docs/spec/launch.md "Release manifest" and "Preview launch");
- a fixture that application tests start a place with (§12.3).

Keyset pages are a store read. They are specified in STORE-SPEC v2.25 §1.5 and in core/std/api/store.md. This section's rules extend §2.1 (V1, V3), §3 and §6, and each extension is marked **extends** where it occurs. No ruling is reopened.

### 12.1 Lifecycle

**One owner.** `runner.operate` owns a place from store open to store close. It replaces the sequence that place programs and the runner's harnesses wire by hand today (`store.open`, `place_head`, `mailbox`, `serve`), so the sequence exists once.

```hirsel
pub type Step: send = Recovering | Activating | Publishing | Listening(Addr)
pub type Phase: send = Starting(Step) | Ready | Draining
pub type Status: shareable + send { phase: Phase, digest: Bytes, target: String, n_place: u64, release: Bytes, addr: Option[Addr] }
pub type Stop: codec + send { lag_ms: u64, grace_ms: u64 }
pub type Backing: send = Memory | Disk(Dir, store.Options) | Temp(Dir)
pub type PlaceConfig[G: send, H: send]: send {
    backing: Backing, place: StaticFn[fn(mut Registry[G, H]) -> Result[(), RegisterError]], n_place: u64,
    handles: StaticFn[fn(mut store.Db) -> Result[H, store.Error] waits], query_handles: StaticFn[fn(H) -> H],
    session: WireConfig, max_sessions: u64, proxies: List[std.wsock.Prefix], policy: ingress.Ingress, n_publisher: u64,
    listen: Addr, net: Net, tasks: Tasks, clock: Clock, random: Random, stop: Stop,
}
pub type StartError: send = Open(OpenError) | Listen(NetError) | Missing(String) | Full(Capacity)   // OpenError is place_head's: Store | Place
pub type Ended: codec + send = Stopped | Forced(u64)

pub fn status_root() -> Result[Latest[Status], Capacity]
pub fn operate[G: send, H: send](take p: PlaceConfig[G, H], take stop: Receiver[()], status: Latest[Status], take watch: Option[Sender[Receiver[Phase]]]) -> Result[Ended, StartError] waits
pub fn release_digest(exported: List[Exported]) -> Result[Bytes, Capacity]
```

- `operate` refuses `grace_ms = 0` with `Missing("grace_ms")` before it opens anything. A zero bound is a bound that was not given, as in STORE-SPEC W1. Admission closes at min(t₀ + `lag_ms`, D), so a lag that reaches the grace needs no refusal.
- `Temp(d)` uses `std.dir.create_temp` for a fresh subdirectory of `d`. The head removes a named directory after it closes or drops the store. Cancellation at directory creation's delivery point can leave a directory whose name was never received (the `host.mkdtemp` cancellation prefix). `Disk` takes the store directory itself, already narrowed by the caller (for launch, the `--dir` grant), and removes nothing.
- Managed wire calls retain W3's behavior: `Granted` is refused as `NotAllowed`. `PlaceConfig` carries no grant for these calls.

**Status.** `digest`, `target` and `n_place` are the launch identity (launch.md). `release` is `release_digest(exported)`, which is SHA-256 over `u8 17 ‖ "hirsel.release.v1" ‖` the value codec's bytes of this registry's `List[Exported]` in registration order. It is empty until activation has committed. The preview child computes `release` with the same function for the manifest (§12.2). `addr` is the bound address, present from `Listening` on. `Step` and `Phase` are send-only: status and watch stay in-process, and the status route writes its own JSON.

`status` is a `Latest[Status]` root (CONC-13). The caller makes it with `status_root()`, which holds `Starting(Recovering)` with the launch identity, an empty `release` and no `addr`, so a read before `operate` starts sees the first phase. `operate` is the only task that calls `latest.publish` on it: it publishes a new value at each phase change. Every other holder gets its own handle with `latest.handle`: S passes one to the route, and the fixture keeps one. Readers call `latest.read` and get the newest value. A read never waits and never consumes, so any number of readers see the same newest phase. In one reader's program order, phases never go backwards.

**Waiting for a phase.** A reader that must wait for a phase, rather than sample it, passes a one-use receiver handoff. `operate` creates its own channel of capacity 6 and offers the receiver on the handoff before it publishes a phase. After each publish, `operate` sends the same `Phase` on the channel it created. It publishes at most six phases (four steps, `Ready`, `Draining`), so that channel never fills. A closed handoff or watch is ignored. `runner.main` passes `None`; the fixture takes the receiver and waits on it (§12.3).

**Tasks.** `operate` runs in the caller's task as the place's supervisor. It waits only on its own inbox, as http.md's supervisor does (HTTP-1). It spawns these children:
- **The head (H).** It opens the store, so it owns the `Db` (P2: own, not send). It runs `handles` and activation, spawns the publisher as its own child, and then serves its inbox, as `place_head` does today. It sends its progress to `operate`: each step, then `Published(Sender[PubIn])`.
- **The serve supervisor (S).** It owns the sessions. It spawns one acceptor child (A) that holds the listener and sends `Accepted` to S. Sessions are S's children, not A's, so A can be cancelled without cancelling a session. This is HTTP-1's split of acceptor and supervisor, applied to `runner.serve`.
- **A stop watcher (W).** It waits on `stop`, then sends `StopRequested` to `operate`.
- **A drain timer (T).** Spawned at the stop, it sends `AdmissionClose` at min(t₀ + `lag_ms`, D) and `Expired` at D, each with `host.sleep_until`. So `operate`'s timers are events on its inbox like every other.

Each child sends one last message to `operate` before it ends.

**Starting.** The steps run in this order. `operate` publishes `Recovering`, `Activating` and `Publishing` before starting each step. It stays in `Starting(Publishing)` during the bind, and publishes `Starting(Listening(addr))` only after bind succeeds. The `Listening` step always carries the actual bound address. H runs the steps in lockstep with `operate`: after each of its steps, H sends the step's result and waits on its inbox for `Continue` or `Close`. So a stop during `Starting` is taken between steps (below). A failure in any step returns its `StartError` after the earlier steps' resources are released in reverse order. For the head, that means the head ends: it drops the publisher (cancel and join, CONC-8) and then calls `store.close`.

| Step | Run by | What it waits on | Failure | What a crash in the step leaves |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `Recovering` | H | `store.open` (recovery, STORE-SPEC §3.4) | `Open(Store(e))` | what §3.6 says for that cut; the next open recovers again |
| `Activating` | H | `handles` once and derives query handles (rule 12), then the activation table's `create_table` on first open, the activation commit (§6 step 3), then the gone-key set | `Open(Place(e))`, `Open(Store(e))` | L13's two outcomes, (old previous, old current) or (old current, new); the next open repeats step 3 or writes nothing |
| `Publishing` | H | the publisher spawned, and its first base taken (the durable version at open) | `Full(c)` | nothing new on disk: activation is already committed, and the next open on the same code writes nothing (§6) |
| `Listening` | `operate` | `host.listen` on `listen` | `Listen(e)` | as `Publishing` |

**Ready.** After `Listening`, S reserves its resources and parks A before its first `accept`. Once S reports that it is prepared, `operate` publishes `Ready` and lets A accept. No session exists before `Ready`, and `Ready` means that activation has committed for this registry's `release`. A probe that connects between the bind and `Ready` waits in the kernel backlog and is answered after `Ready`.

A failure after the activation commit and before `Ready` leaves the new activation rows on disk, because it happens in `Publishing` or `Listening`. The process never reported `Ready`, so a confirmation fails (§12.2). An operator who rolls back to the old code meets D2-K's rollback row (§6), which is unchanged.

**A stop during `Starting`.** `operate` takes `StopRequested` in any phase. During `Starting`, it fixes D = t₀ + `grace_ms` as below, publishes `Draining`, spawns T, and answers H's next step result with `Close`. Nothing has been accepted yet, so there is nothing to refuse.
- H ends as a failure in that step ends: it drops the publisher if it spawned one and calls `store.close` if it opened the store. `operate` releases the listener if it bound one, and returns `Ok(Stopped)`.
- A step still running at D (a long recovery) is not waited for: at `Expired`, `operate` cancels H and joins it. H is cancelled at its next cancellation point inside the step, and the `Db`, if it exists, is dropped without `close` (§1.10, §3.7). `operate` returns `Ok(Forced(0))`.
- `Ready` is never published after a stop is taken. If S has already prepared, its acceptor is released without accepting and S is joined.

**The stop input.** `stop` is an ordinary channel. W takes the first `()` on it, or its `Closed`, as the stop request. Who sends it is the caller's choice: `runner.main` connects it to the host stop source (below), and the fixture sends it at its end (§12.3).

**Draining.** `operate` takes `StopRequested` at t₀ (`host.mono_ns`). The drain deadline is D = t₀ + `grace_ms`, absolute, and it is the only deadline of the drain.
1. **Withdraw readiness.** `operate` publishes `Draining` and spawns T. Until admission closes, sessions and connections are served as before, so a proxy that polls readiness stops routing before anything is refused.
2. **Close admission** when `operate` receives T's `AdmissionClose`, at min(t₀ + `lag_ms`, D):
   - `operate` sends `Close(D)` to S and to H's inbox. Both inboxes are bounded and always drained, so each `send` waits for at most one message's handling.
   - S cancels A and joins it. That drops the listener, and the host then refuses new connections. S then sends `Close(D)` to each session.
   - Under std.http, the same step is http.md HTTP-1's "Stopping" (v2.15) with deadline D. One D serves both transports.
3. **Sessions after `Close(D)` (extends V1).**
   - A send the session does not already hold is answered `Refused(Overloaded)` at once. Nothing is recorded and nothing is forwarded.
   - A send of a position the session holds is a join (V2), as before, and waits for the head's answer.
   - A `Subscribe` is answered `Ended(sub, Refused(Overloaded))`.
   - A compute call in progress in the owner task finishes there under its step bound (P1), and is answered as usual.
   - Every owed send's deadline becomes min(its own, D).
   - `Overloaded` already means "nothing recorded; re-send the same p", which is what a client of a draining place must do, at another instance or after a restart. So no Refusal tag is added, and frame-vectors.json is unchanged.
4. **The head after `Close` (extends V3).**
   - A running worker keeps running. Its group commits, waits on `durable` and is released as H2–H3 say.
   - A request in a stream's FIFO that was never admitted is answered `Refused(Overloaded)` as V3, in a snapshot-less Release.
   - A request taken after `Close` is answered the same way, unless it joins a running worker with an equal request digest (H1). Then it waits for that worker.
   - Nothing that reached `admit` is answered by the drain.
   - When the head holds no worker and no FIFO entry, it sends `Settled` to `operate` and keeps serving its inbox, which can only bring more `Overloaded` answers or joins.
5. **Sessions end.** A session that owes no send ends. A WebSocket session closes with 1001 (going away). Its subscriptions end with it through its one end path (v2.14: `Left` or the mark), and no `Ended` is sent for them; the client re-subscribes elsewhere. S joins each session as it ends, and once it has none it sends `Done(0)` to `operate` and ends.
6. **Finish.** When `operate` holds `Settled` and S's `Done`, it sends `Finish` to H. H sends `Halt` to the publisher, joins it, calls `store.close` and ends. Sessions are gone before `Halt`, so every Release the head made has reached its sessions. `operate` joins W, T, S and H, and returns `Ok(Stopped)`.

**At D (extends V1).**
- Each send still owed at D is logically `Abandoned`. A session applies this cut before processing any post-D event. The server can attempt to deliver the answers and Close 1001, but the forced path cancels the writer instead of waiting for delivery. A peer that is not reading observes a disconnect and retries under H4. S sends `Done(n)`, where n is the number of sends its sessions settled as `Abandoned` at D, and ends.
- `operate` then cancels H, if H has not ended, and joins it.
  - H is cancelled at its next cancellation point (CONC-7). That is the `durable` wait, an inbox `receive`, or the end of a running body. A running body ends by its step bound `s` (D5), so the join waits at most one body's step bound plus the shielded joins of the store's tasks (STORE-SPEC §1.10).
  - H's cleanup cancels and joins the publisher (CONC-8). It drops the `Db` without `close`: §1.10 "Drop without close" and §3.7 leave the store at the crash cut of that moment, and the next open recovers it (§3.6).
  - An admitted, unreplied position comes back as `Abandoned` and runs again (H4).
- `operate` returns `Ok(Forced(n))`. So a forced exit adds nothing that L1 and H4 do not already cover.

**Poison** is unchanged: if `durable` returns an error, the head answers nothing and the process exits, in any phase.

**What a crash leaves at each point.** A crash in `Ready`, or in `Draining` before or after `Close`, is the crash L1 already covers: every Outcome a client saw that reports a commit is durable, and every other position is re-sent and answered. The drain writes nothing of its own, so it adds no cut.

**Replay.** The stop request is a channel receive. t₀ and every deadline come from `host.mono_ns`. Both are events at the replay point (CONC-10, CAP-8). The host stop source is a provider result (below). So a replay takes the stop at the same point, reaches the same D and records the same answers.

**The host stop source** is a new host operation, and Sam's call (§12.4).
- `host.stopped(p: Process) waits` returns once the process has received its first SIGTERM or SIGINT.
- It is a cancellation point at its entry and while it waits. Its completion passes the replay point at issue and at delivery (CAP-8).
- The runtime installs the handler only when the program holds `Process`. A program without it keeps the default action, which ends the process as a crash cut.
- A second SIGTERM or SIGINT after the first ends the process at once, also as a crash cut.
- `runner.main` spawns one task that calls `host.stopped` and then drops the stop `Sender`, so W reads `Closed`.
- `main` returns `Ok(())` for `Stopped`. For `Forced(n)` and for a `StartError` it writes one stderr line and returns `Error(Exit(status: 1u8))`. The line is `place: forced at the drain deadline, n sends abandoned`, or the error.

**Readiness is read through a status route on the place's own listener.** The place serves `GET /hirsel/status` as a raw route (HTTP-11), beside `GET /hirsel` (http.md §6).
- The handler calls `latest.read(status)`. It answers 200 when the phase is `Ready` and 503 when it is `Draining`.
- The body is one JSON object, with its keys in this order:
  - `phase`: `"ready"` or `"draining"`;
  - `digest`: lowercase hex, empty when unavailable;
  - `target`;
  - `n_place`;
  - `release`: lowercase hex.
- No connection is accepted before `Ready`, so the route never sees `Starting`. The route records nothing and admits nothing. It is answered before the place's ingress policy, which admits sessions only.

**Why the route, and not a supervisor channel:**
- The readers of readiness are outside the process: the proxy that routes to the place, the supervisor that restarts it, and the release confirmation in §12.2. Each of them already speaks an HTTP probe. A supervisor channel would need a second protocol that reaches outside the process for the same fact: a control socket, so a second listener and a second grant.
- Every served place already has the raw route path, so the status route is a second route of a kind the place already serves.
- A probe that crosses the listener also shows that the listener accepts, which a side channel cannot show.
- The in-process source still exists: it is `status`, the one record of the phase. The route renders it, and the fixture reads it.

**What a reader sees.** Before the bind, a probe sees a refused connection. Between the bind and `Ready`, it waits in the backlog and then gets 200. From `Draining` until admission closes, it gets 503 `"draining"`. After admission closes, it sees a refused connection again. So "not ready" is either a 503 or a refused connection, and only `Ready` is ever a 200.

**Until std.http serves the place,** the place listens through `runner.serve`'s WebSocket accept path, which has no router. In that interval, the lifecycle and its `Latest` are built and tested in process (§12.3), and the route is built with http.md §6's cutover. That is build order, and SLICES.md records it.

### 12.2 Release manifest and preview

**The manifest** is the release's record. `hirsel build --manifest PATH` writes it after it links the executable. It runs the built executable once in the preview launch with an empty old list (launch.md), through the same `describe_child` helper, with the same deadline and bound. So:
- the entry list and `release` come from running `place` inside the executable, and from nowhere else;
- `release` has one implementation, `runner.release_digest`.

That **extends D1** ("it runs `place`") to the manifest: there is no second entry list. The manifest's contents and encoding are in launch.md "Release manifest": the executable's digest and target, `n_place`, the exported entries, `release`, the exact grants and the root limit.

**The preview** is read-only. `hirsel preview --exe NEW NEW.manifest OLD.manifest` compares the release to be run with the running one.
- It refuses an executable whose launch digest differs from NEW's (launch.md).
- It runs NEW in the preview launch mode, which opens no store and no listener.
- The child runs `place` and answers each of OLD's exported entries with **the session's own answer to a Hello `Want`**. That code is extracted from the session's `Placed` path (runner.hirsel, the `Placed(take answer)` arm) as `answer_want`, and the session calls it after the change. So the two cannot differ. The answers are:
  - `Ready`: unchanged;
  - `Resolve`: resolvable. When R4 builds `Resolve`, the preview gains it with no change;
  - `Gone`: gone, so a client that holds it gets `Gone` + `Upgrade`.
- Entries of NEW whose key matches no entry of OLD are new.
- The preview also reports changed grants (a set difference of the canonical grant strings), and a changed target, root limit or `n_place`.
- It reports **schema readiness: unknown**. Entry compatibility is not schema readiness. Under D2-K, a changed row schema can make the new `handles` fail with `Schema` at open, and only the target store's catalog can say whether it will. That catalog belongs to the running place, which holds the store. A catalog-aware preview needs a read-only catalog view from another process, which does not exist, so the preview says unknown and never guesses.
- The preview gates nothing (ENDSTATE: "trials and diffs are tools"). It exits 0 whatever it finds. It exits 2 only when it cannot answer: a child error, a timeout or an unreadable manifest.

**"The running release"** is the manifest whose identity the running place confirms. The preview compares two manifest files, and the confirmation below ties OLD.manifest to what is running, so no second remote read is needed.

**Confirmation through readiness.** `hirsel confirm MANIFEST ADDR` sends `GET /hirsel/status` to ADDR under an absolute deadline: 10 s by default, set by `--confirm-timeout`. The answer is one of these:
- `Confirmed`: the phase is `Ready`, and `digest`, `target`, `n_place` and `release` all equal the manifest's;
- `NotReady(phase)` for a 503, or not listening for a refused connection;
- `Different(fields)`: `Ready`, with at least one available field that differs;
- `Unknown`: `Ready`, with an empty digest on either side (launch.md, "identity unavailable") and the other fields equal. An unavailable identity is never reported as a mismatch.

`confirm` exits 0 only for `Confirmed`. A supervisor publishes a release with its own tools, then runs `confirm` until it gets `Confirmed` or its own deadline passes. Hirsel runs no rollout.

### 12.3 Place fixture for application tests

`runner.fixture` starts a place in a test with the application's real `place` and `handles`, through `operate`. A test therefore runs the same lifecycle, sessions, head and publisher that production runs. It runs them over the wire path on the granted `Net`, never through the in-process seam (DECISIONS 2026-11-17, "W3e waits on W8a").

```hirsel
pub type FixtureError: send = Start(StartError) | Deadline(u64) | Full(Capacity)
pub type CallError: send = Deadline(u64) | Closed | Protocol(String) | Decode(std.codec.DecodeError) | Full(Capacity)
pub type Answer[R] = Returned(R) | Failed(Fault) | Refused(Refusal) | Abandoned
pub type Replied[R] { answer: Answer[R], version: u64 }
pub type Seen { version: u64, rows: List[Row] }

pub opaque type Fixture: send
pub opaque type Caller: send
pub opaque type Callee[A, R]: shareable + send

pub fn start[G: send, H: send](take p: PlaceConfig[G, H], within_ms: u64) -> Result[Fixture, FixtureError] waits
pub fn compute_entry[A, R](f: StaticFn[fn(take A) -> Result[R, Capacity]]) -> Callee[A, R]
pub fn transact_entry[H, A, R, E](f: StaticFn[fn(mut store.Tx, H, take A) -> Result[R, E]]) -> Callee[A, Result[R, E]]
pub fn query_entry[H, A](f: StaticFn[fn(store.Tx, H, take A) -> Result[Cover, Capacity]]) -> Callee[A, Cover]
pub fn caller(f: Fixture, within_ms: u64) -> Result[Caller, CallError] waits
pub fn call[A: codec, R: codec](mut c: Caller, e: Callee[A, R], take a: A, deadline_ms: u32) -> Result[Replied[R], CallError] waits
pub fn subscribe[A: codec](mut c: Caller, e: Callee[A, Cover], take a: A, within_ms: u64) -> Result[u32, CallError] waits
pub fn seen_at(mut c: Caller, sub: u32, version: u64, within_ms: u64) -> Result[Seen, CallError] waits
pub fn status(f: Fixture) -> Status
pub fn end(take f: Fixture, within_ms: u64) -> Result[Ended, FixtureError] waits
```

**Time.** Every bound is relative and measured on the `Clock` in the fixture's `PlaceConfig` (`host.mono_ns`), as the wire `Call`'s `deadline_ms` is. A wait that reaches its bound is `Deadline(bound)`.

**Start.** `start` requires the backing to be `Memory` or `Temp` and `listen` to be 127.0.0.1 port 0. It refuses anything else with `Start(Missing("backing"))` or `Start(Missing("listen"))`.
- It makes the root with `status_root()`, spawns `operate` in its own task group, and takes the `watch` receiver from the one-use handoff. It receives phases until `Ready`. The fixture keeps a handle to the root for `status`.
- A `StartError` is returned as `Start(e)`.
- If `within_ms` passes first, the result is `Deadline(within_ms)`. It is returned after the fixture has stopped `operate` and released everything it made.

**Typed calls.**
- `compute_entry`, `transact_entry` and `query_entry` compute the entry's key with the function registration uses: identity and the two fingerprints. So a typed `Callee` cannot name another key.
- Granted entries have no constructor. A wire client cannot call one: a Hello names entries for `Call` and `Subscribe` only.
- `caller` connects one wire client to `status.addr`, with a `Hello` that lists the fixture's entries, and owns one stream.
- `call` assigns the next position by WIRE's client rules (P1–P3). It encodes `a` and sends one `Call` with `deadline_ms`. It returns when that send's Outcome row arrives, and V1 makes the place answer by `deadline_ms` (`Abandoned` at the latest). A transact `Returned` is decoded as `Result[R, E]`. A broken connection is `Closed`.

**A durable reply.** `Replied.version` is the version of the Batch that carried the Outcome. By V4, a reply that reports a commit is released only after `durable`, so a `Replied` that `call` returns is already durable at that version. Nothing extra waits.

**The batch at or after a version.** `seen_at(c, sub, v, within_ms)` returns the subscription's full row set as of the first Batch at a version ≥ v. That is L6+L7's count form. It applies Batches in order, as the client cache does. A subscription that is `Ended` gives `Closed`.

**End.** `end` sends the stop and waits for `operate`'s `Ended`. It closes every `Caller` it made, and `operate` removes the `Temp` subdirectory. It returns `Ended`. If `within_ms` passes, the result is `Deadline(within_ms)`, returned after the group is cancelled and joined. **Dropping a `Fixture` without `end`** cancels and joins its task group (CONC-8). That drops the listener and the `Db` (a crash cut, §1.10), and `operate`'s cleanup removes the `Temp` subdirectory. Every resource the fixture made is released on either path.

**Grants.** A test that uses the fixture needs `Net` (listen and connect on 127.0.0.1), `Tasks`, `Clock` and `Random`, plus `Dir` for `Temp`. It receives them as ordinary capability parameters (PROG-4) and places them in `PlaceConfig`.

**Engines.** The fixture runs on Cranelift, LLVM and the evaluator. Evaluator fixtures explicitly grant W8a's model network, which supplies their controlled network and virtual clock. Native fixtures use the granted loopback network.

**The runner's own harness.** `runner_calls.hirsel`'s `served` and `served_on` drive the head over the wire with `wire_client`, using the production `serve_connection` owner. The evaluator runs those same fixtures on its model network. Subscription-pressure laws additionally hold a joined publisher inbox undrained until their drain step. The in-process client/session seam is deleted.

### 12.4 Calls this section makes

- **The host stop source (Sam's call).** It changes what `--grant Process` means at the command line.
  - **Recommended:** `host.stopped(p: Process)`, under the existing `Process` capability. `Process` has no operations in v1, and the end of one's own process is process authority.
  - **The /wholehog alternative:** a new grant kind, `Signal`, which keeps `Process` for starting children (gap 26). It is not recommended, because it adds a kind for one operation.
  - **Reversible** until the slice lands: renaming the capability touches one host operation and the grant table.
- **The status route's contract** (outward-facing: proxies and supervisors will read it). `GET /hirsel/status` with 200/503 and the JSON fields above. Reversible until the route ships in a release.
- **The new CLI verbs** (outward-facing): `hirsel build --manifest`, `hirsel manifest show`, `hirsel preview` and `hirsel confirm`.
- **Readiness through the route** is decided here, for the reasons above. Reversible: the route is one raw handler over `status`.
- **`Overloaded` for drain refusals** is decided here. A `Draining` refusal would append tag 8 and change frame-vectors.json, and it would ask the client for the same action. Reversible until a client needs to tell the two apart.
- **The manifest from the build** is decided here, and extends D1. Reversible: the manifest is one file that one command writes.
